At the time, it felt like I could handle it all, but it's just far more than I can realistically manage. Now all these deadlines are hitting at once, and the mortgage is due, and it's all just starting to close in on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don't know if anxious is the right word. It's more just... overwhelming. I mean, the deadlines and the bills are very real, so I'm definitely stressed about how I'm actually going to get everything done. But I wouldn't say I'm having anxiety or anything like that, just feeling the heavy pressure of the mess I made.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any activities or tasks because of your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anything. I mean... I have to get this stuff done, so I'm still in the studio every day trying to chip away at the list. It's just that there are only so many hours in the day, so things are naturally falling behind. But I'm not hiding from the work or putting it off on purpose, I just physically can't keep up with it all right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad. Um, maybe just a little frustrated with myself for overcommitting, but not really down or depressed. It really is just the stress of the actual workload I'm dealing with right now, not a sadness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm tired at the end of the day, but just the normal amount, you know? It's not like an unusual exhaustion or anything. I mean, I think about the work I have to do because it's right in front of me, but I'm not obsessing over it or anything. It's just... practical thoughts about what needs to get done next. Um, I haven't been avoiding any places or situations. I still go to my studio every day, run my errands, pick up supplies... I haven't felt uncomfortable or upset anywhere, so there's really been no reason to stay away from anything. I'm just sticking to my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself performing certain rituals or habits over and over?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have my daily routine, like how I set up my paints and brushes before I start working, but it's just how I organize things. I don't feel like I have to do it over and over or anything like that. Just normal habits, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Now all these deadlines are hitting at once, and the mortgage is due, and it's all just starting to close in on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about your situation?\n\nPatient: I don't know if anxious is the right word. It's more just... overwhelming. I mean, the deadlines and the bills are very real, so I'm definitely stressed about how I'm actually going to get everything done. But I wouldn't say I'm having anxiety or anything like that, just feeling the heavy pressure of the mess I made.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any activities or tasks because of your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding anything. I mean... I have to get this stuff done, so I'm still in the studio every day trying to chip away at the list. It's just that there are only so many hours in the day, so things are naturally falling behind. But I'm not hiding from the work or putting it off on purpose, I just physically can't keep up with it all right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad. Um, maybe just a little frustrated with myself for overcommitting, but not really down or depressed. It really is just the stress of the actual workload I'm dealing with right now, not a sadness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm tired at the end of the day, but just the normal amount, you know? It's not like an unusual exhaustion or anything. I'm just working a lot, so I'm naturally a bit worn out by the evening, but I wouldn't say I'm unusually tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I think about the work I have to do because it's right in front of me, but I'm not obsessing over it or anything. It's just... practical thoughts about what needs to get done next. I wouldn't call it worrying, just planning, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or places you've been avoiding lately because they make you feel uncomfortable or upset?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Um, I haven't been avoiding any places or situations. I still go to my studio every day, run my errands, pick up supplies... I haven't felt uncomfortable or upset anywhere, so there's really been no reason to stay away from anything. I'm just sticking to my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself performing certain rituals or habits over and over?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I have my daily routine, like how I set up my paints and brushes before I start working, but it's just how I organize things. I don't feel like I have to do it over and over or anything like that. Just normal habits, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep coming back to you even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mind is pretty focused on whatever I'm working on at the time. I don't have thoughts that just pop up and won't go away. If I'm trying to concentrate on something else, I can usually just focus on that. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors in order to feel right or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. Um, I don't have any actions I feel forced to repeat. Like I mentioned with my art supplies, I organize them, but it's definitely not to prevent something bad from happening or to make myself feel right. It's just practical. So, no, I don't have any behaviors like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you cannot control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Um, like I said before, my mind stays pretty focused on my work. I don't have thoughts that get stuck or that I can't control. If I want to think about something else, I just do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like headaches, stomach issues, or muscle tension since you've been feeling overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything physical like that. No headaches or stomach problems, and my muscles feel fine. I guess I've just been so focused on everything I'm doing that physically, I feel okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any trouble sleeping. Um, I actually sleep pretty well. When I'm done working for the day, I just go to bed and I don't really have any issues falling asleep or staying asleep. So, no, my sleep is fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel stressed when you're interacting with other people, like clients or at workshops?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel stressed around people. When I'm talking to clients or doing a workshop, I just do my thing. It's just part of the work, so it doesn't really bother me or make me anxious. I just handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're at your breaking point with all the pressure you're under?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like I'm at a breaking point. I mean, I'm busy, sure, but I'm handling it. I don't feel overwhelmed or like I can't take it anymore. I'm just getting my work done.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel emotionally drained after interacting with clients or attendees at your workshops?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel drained. I mean, it's just work. I talk to people, I do the workshops, and then I go home. It doesn't really take that much out of me emotionally. I just kind of do what I need to do and move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped enjoying activities you used to find pleasurable because of your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I still enjoy the things I usually do. I mean, my art and just my regular hobbies... they still feel the same to me. I wouldn't say I've lost interest or stopped enjoying them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any social interactions because they might make you feel uncomfortable or upset?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been avoiding anyone. I still see my friends and talk to people just like I normally would. I don't feel uncomfortable around them or anything like that. Socializing is pretty much the same as it's always been for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depleted of your mental or physical resources due to your prolonged workload?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depleted. I mean, I have a lot going on with my art and the workshops, but it's just my normal routine. I don't feel physically or mentally exhausted by it. I just handle the workload as it comes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
